*Proposition 5.4.14 and microlocal Morse lemma again we have

Hom(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\pi_{M}^{-1}\pi_{M!}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}[1])\simeq\Gamma(M\times(-a,+\infty),\mathscr{H}om(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},T_{-a^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}))\simeq Hom(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},T_{-a^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}).

However since
T_{-a^{\prime}}\widetilde{L}\subset T^{*,\infty}_{\tau<0}(M\times\mathbb{R})
, following
[
39
]
*Proposition 3.16 we will conclude that
Hom(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},T_{-a^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\simeq Hom(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})
, which gives the full faithfulness.

Finally, consider the extended sheaf
j_{M\times\mathbb{R},*}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\in\operatorname{Sh}_{\widetilde{L}}(M\times[-\infty,+\infty]).
Write
\overline{\pi}_{M}:M\times[-\infty,+\infty]\rightarrow M
for the proper projection and
i_{c}:M\times[c,+\infty]\hookrightarrow M\times[-\infty,+\infty]
for the closed embedding. Then by microlocal Morse lemma, we know that

\pi_{M!}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\simeq\overline{\pi}_{M!}j_{M\times\mathbb{R},*}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\simeq\overline{\pi}_{M!}\,i_{c!}\,i_{c}^{-1}\,(j_{M\times\mathbb{R},*}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\simeq i_{+\infty}^{-1}(j_{M\times\mathbb{R},*}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}).

This completes the proof.
∎

Remark B.21
.

The sheaf quantization functor in Theorem
B.20
can be naturally restricted to pseudoperfect objects, in other words sheaves with perfect stalks,

\operatorname{Loc}_{\epsilon}^{pp}(\widetilde{L})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Sh}^{pp}_{\widetilde{L}}(M\times[-\infty,+\infty])\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Sh}^{pp}_{\Lambda}(M).

In addition, by passing to the left adjoints, as in Appendix
A
., we are able to obtain a functor, which is the sheaf theoretic Viterbo restriction functor

\operatorname{Sh}^{cpt}_{\Lambda}(M)\rightarrow\operatorname{Sh}^{cpt}_{\widetilde{L}}(M\times[-\infty,+\infty])\rightarrow\operatorname{Loc}_{\epsilon}^{cpt}(\widetilde{L}).

Moreover, we can show the Hamiltonian invariance of the sheaf quantization in the same spirit as
[
39
]
*Section 3.19-20.

Recall that given a family of Lagrangian fillings that are conical at infinity, Lemma
B.19
ensures that there exists a Hamiltonian flow such that the image of the family of exact Lagrangians will have primitives that are proper and bounded from below.

Proposition B.22
.

Let
L\subset(T^{*}M,\lambda_{st})
be a relatively spin exact Lagrangian filling of a Legendrian submanifold
\Lambda\subset T^{*,\infty}M
,
\widetilde{L}\subset(J^{1}M,\xi_{st})
its Legendrian lift and
\mathcal{L}\in\operatorname{Loc}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{I}_{\widetilde{L}})
a twisted local system. Consider a Hamiltonian
H:T^{*}M\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
that is homogeneous at infinity,
\varphi_{t}\in\mbox{Ham}(T^{*}M,d\lambda_{st})
its time-
t
flow,
t\in[0,1]
, which extends to an homonymous contactomorphism
\varphi_{t}\in\mbox{Cont}(T^{*,\infty}M,\ker\lambda_{st})
of the ideal contact boundary,
t\in[0,1]
,
\widetilde{H}=\Pi^{*}_{M}(H):J^{1}M\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
the pull-back and
\widetilde{\varphi}_{t}\in\mbox{Cont}(J^{1}M,\xi_{st})
its time-
t
flow.

Suppose that
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\in\operatorname{Sh}_{\widetilde{L}_{t}}(M\times\mathbb{R})
is a sheaf quantization of
(\varphi_{t}(L),(\varphi_{t})_{*}\mathcal{L})
and consider the proper push-forward
\mathcal{F}_{t}:=\pi_{M,!}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t})\in\operatorname{Sh}_{\varphi_{t}(\Lambda)}(M)
,
t\in[0,1]
. Then:

(i)

The sheaf kernel convolution
\Phi_{\widetilde{H}}:\operatorname{Sh}_{\widetilde{L}}(M\times\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sh}_{\varphi_{1}(\widetilde{L})}(M\times\mathbb{R})
associated to
\widetilde{H}
satisfies
\Phi_{\widetilde{H}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{0})\simeq\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
.

(ii)

The sheaf kernel convolution
\Phi_{H}:\operatorname{Sh}_{\Lambda}(M)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sh}_{\varphi_{1}(\Lambda)}(M)
associated
H
satisfies
\Phi_{H}(\mathcal{F}_{0})\simeq\mathcal{F}_{1}.

Proof.

Consider the Legendrian isotopy of the Legendrian lifts
\widetilde{L}_{t}
,
t\in[0,1]
, induced by the contact Hamiltonian
\widetilde{H}
. We have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Loc}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{I}_{\widetilde{L}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Loc}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{I}_{\widetilde{L}\times[0,1]})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{0}^{-1}\hskip 15.0pt}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 15.0pti_{1}^{-1}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Loc}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{I}_{\widetilde{L}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Sh}_{\widetilde{L}_{0}}(M\times(-\infty,+\infty))}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Sh}_{\widetilde{L}_{\widetilde{H}}}(M\times(-\infty,+\infty)\times[0,1])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{0}^{-1}\hskip 15.0pt}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 15.0pti_{1}^{-1}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Sh}_{\widetilde{L}_{1}}(M\times(-\infty,+\infty)).}

Note that the composition in the first row is the identity, while the composition in the second row is
\Phi_{\widetilde{H}}
. Therefore we know that
\Phi_{\widetilde{H}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{0})\simeq\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{1}.

Next, consider the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Sh}_{\widetilde{L}_{0}}(M\times(-\infty,+\infty))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{M!}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Sh}_{\widetilde{L}_{\widetilde{H}}}(M\times(-\infty,+\infty)\times[0,1])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{0}^{-1}\hskip 15.0pt}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 15.0pti_{1}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{M\times[0,1]!}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Sh}_{\widetilde{L}_{1}}(M\times(-\infty,+\infty))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{M!}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Sh}_{\Lambda_{0}}(M)}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Sh}_{\Lambda_{H}}(M\times[0,1])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{0}^{-1}\hskip 15.0pt}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 15.0pti_{1}^{-1}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Sh}_{\Lambda_{1}}(M).}

Note that the composition in the first row is
\Phi_{\widetilde{H}}
, while the composition in the second row is
\Phi_{H}
. By proper base change formula we have

\Phi_{H}(\mathcal{F}_{0})=\Phi_{H}(\pi_{M!}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{0})\simeq\pi_{M!}(\Phi_{\widetilde{H}}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{0})\simeq\pi_{M!}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{1}=\mathcal{F}_{1},

which completes the proof.

Finally, we remark that sheaves in
\operatorname{Sh}_{\widetilde{L}_{t}}(M\times\mathbb{R})
can be naturally extended to
\operatorname{Sh}_{\widetilde{L}_{0}}(M\times[-\infty,+\infty])
, and we can still get the commutative diagram as above.
∎

Remark B.23
.

There is another sheaf quantization functor following
[
49
]

\operatorname{Loc}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{I}_{L})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Sh}_{\Lambda}(M)
independent of the work of S. Guillermou and X. Jin-D. Treumann. In general, we do not know whether the two constructions coincide. However, in all examples in this paper, one can verify that the two constructions yield the same result.

B.4.
Moduli space and framed sheaves

The microlocal rank is a discrete piece of data that can be specified to choose components in the derived stack of pseudo-perfect objects in
\operatorname{Sh}_{\Lambda}^{cpt}(M)
. In fact, we also study the moduli space of sheaves with additional framing data. In this section, we will interpret framing data from different perspectives, focusing on the case of Legendrian links. Given an exact Lagrangian filling
L
, not necessarily embedded, with Legendrian lift
\widetilde{L}
, we will also define microlocal merodromy functions of framed sheaves with singular support on
\widetilde{L}
the along relative 1-cycles.

B.4.1.
Moduli space of framed microlocal sheaves

The first moduli space that we focused on is defined as follows:

Definition B.24
.

Let
\Sigma
be a surface with marked points
36
36

36

In the whole section, marked points always mean the punctures on the surface. In Legendrian contact homology, people also use the term marked points for a different meaning. We will call those points base points to avoid confusion.
and
\Lambda\subset T^{*,\infty}\Sigma
a non-empty Legendrian link equipped with Maslov potential. By definition, the derived stack
\mathbb{R}\mathcal{M}(\Sigma,\Lambda)
is the derived moduli stack of the finite type dg-category
\operatorname{Sh}^{cpt}_{\Lambda}(\Sigma)
, as defined in
[
68
]
, which parametrizes pseudoperfect modules
\operatorname{Sh}^{pp}_{\Lambda}(\Sigma)
.

Let
\mathbb{R}\mathcal{M}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
be the locus in
\mathbb{R}\mathcal{M}(\Sigma,\Lambda)
parametrizing microlocal rank
r
sheaves in
\operatorname{Sh}_{\Lambda}^{pp}(\Sigma)
with acyclic stalks at the marked points. Then the Artin stack
\mathcal{M}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
is the 1-rigid locus of the truncated derived moduli of objects
t_{0}\mathbb{R}\mathcal{M}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
.

The fact that
\operatorname{Sh}^{cpt}_{\Lambda}(\Sigma)
is a smooth dg-category when
\Lambda
is subanalytic isotropic is proved in
[
24
, Section 4.5]
; see also Appendix
A
.

Remark B.25
.

The moduli
\mathcal{M}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
only parametrizes microlocal rank
r
sheaves with singular support on
\Lambda
with no negative self extensions, as it is truncated and the 1-rigid locus is selected. For instance, the
m(5_{2})
Legendrian knot with maximal Thurston-Bennequin invariant that does not have a binary Maslov potential supports microlocal rank 1 sheaves with negative self extensions
[
66
]
*Proposition 7.6; these are not parametrized by that above Artin stack.

From the sheaf quantization result Theorem
B.20
, see Remark
B.21
, for an exact Lagrangian filling
L
of
\Lambda
we have the left adjoint to the fully faithful sheaf quantization functor

\operatorname{Sh}^{cpt}_{\Lambda}(\Sigma)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sh}^{cpt}_{\widetilde{L}}(\Sigma\times\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Loc}^{cpt}(\widetilde{L}),

as is explained in Appendix
A
. Therefore, due to the full faithfulness, we obtain an open embedding of moduli spaces by
[
68
, Theorem 0.2]
, see also
[
65
, Proposition 2.15]
, which reads as follows:

Proposition B.26
.

Let
L\subset T^{*}\Sigma_{\text{op}}
be an exact Lagrangian filling of
\Lambda\subset T^{*,\infty}\Sigma
. Then there is an open embedding of moduli spaces
\mathbb{R}\mathcal{L}oc(L)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathcal{M}(\Sigma,\Lambda)
and
\mathcal{L}oc_{r}(L)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
.

The framed analogues are discussed as follows. First, we define the (micro)framed moduli space using trivializations of microstalks at a point instead of sheaves locally defined near a point. This is closer in relation to both the definition of base points in Legendrian contact homology and the definition of decorated moduli space of sheaves of
[
9
]
*Definition 2.37. We introduce the following new definition:

Definition B.27
.

A microframing
(T,t_{T})
of microlocal rank
r
sheaves in
\mathcal{M}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
consists of a finite subset
T\subset\Lambda
and a rank
r
local system
t_{T}\in\mathcal{L}oc_{r}(T)
. The microframed moduli space of microlocal rank
r
sheaves with singular support in
\Lambda
with respect to the framing
(T,\mathcal{F}_{T})
is the fiber product

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}^{\mu,\textit{fr}}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m_{\Lambda,T}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spec}\,\Bbbk\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t_{T}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}oc_{r}(T).}

Remark B.28
.

The map between moduli spaces
\mathcal{M}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}oc_{r}(T)
is induced by the left adjoint of the microlocalization functor and restriction functor (see Appendix
A
)

m_{\Lambda,T}^{L}:\operatorname{Loc}^{cpt}(T)\rightarrow\operatorname{Loc}^{cpt}(\Lambda)\rightarrow\operatorname{Sh}_{\Lambda}^{cpt}(M).

Remark B.29
.

When
\Lambda
is equipped with a relative spin structure, or equivalently a section
s
in
\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}}
over the 2-skeleton, then strictly speaking, the microframing is defined over the section at
T
as
t_{T}\in\mathcal{L}oc_{r}(s({T}))
.

Similarly, given an exact Lagrangian filling
L
of
\Lambda
, consider the moduli space of (micro)framed local systems
\mathcal{L}oc_{r}^{\textit{fr}}(L)
as the fiber product

\textstyle{\mathcal{L}oc^{\textit{fr}}_{r}(L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}oc_{r}(L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spec}\,\Bbbk\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t_{T}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}oc_{r}(T).}

Then the sheaf quantization result Theorem
B.20
and Proposition
B.26
asserts the following open embedding of (micro)framed moduli spaces.

Corollary B.30
.

Let
L\subset T^{*}\Sigma_{\text{op}}
be an exact Lagrangian filling of
\Lambda\subset T^{*,\infty}\Sigma
. Then there is an open embedding of moduli spaces
\mathcal{L}oc^{\textit{fr}}_{r}(L)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{r}^{\mu,\textit{fr}}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
.

Here we use the terminology of microframings in order to distinguish them from the definition of framing data in
[
65
]
*Section 2.4, which reads as follows:

Definition B.31

(
[
65
]
*Definition 2.19)
.

A framing
(U,\mathcal{F}_{U})
of microlocal rank
r
sheaves in
\mathcal{M}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
consists of an open subset
U\subset\Sigma
and a microlocal rank
r
sheaf
\mathcal{F}_{U}\in\mathcal{M}_{r}(U,\Lambda\cap T^{*,\infty}U)_{0}
. The framed moduli space of microlocal rank
r
sheaves with singular support in
\Lambda
with respect to the framing
(U,\mathcal{F}_{U})
is the fiber product

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}^{\textit{fr}}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spec}\,\Bbbk\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{U}\hskip 30.0pt}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{r}(U,\Lambda\cap T^{*,\infty}U)_{0}.}

Remark B.32
.

We can always consider a sufficiently small contractible open subset
U\subset\Sigma
containing
\pi(T)
, choose
\mathcal{F}_{U}
such that
m_{\Lambda,P}(\mathcal{F}_{U})=\mathcal{F}_{T}
, and ask for these framing data and microframing data whether
\mathcal{M}^{\textit{fr},\mu}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}\simeq\mathcal{M}^{\textit{fr}}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
. In general, this is false. To wit, we consider the right-handed Legendrian trefoil with maximal Thurston-Bennequin number whose front projection admits a Maslov potential with values
-1,0,1
, and let
p\in\Lambda
as in Figure
65
. Then

\mathcal{M}^{\textit{fr}}_{r}(\mathbb{D}^{2},\Lambda)_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{r}(\mathbb{D}^{2},\Lambda)_{0}

is not surjective, while
\mathcal{M}^{\mu,\textit{fr}}_{r}(\mathbb{D}^{2},\Lambda)_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{r}(\mathbb{D}^{2},\Lambda)_{0}
is surjective.

Figure 65.
The right-handed Legendrian trefoil with maximal Thurston-Bennequin number whose front projection admits a Maslov potential with values
-1,0,1
. The restrictions of two sheaves in the grey region
U
around
\pi(p)\in\pi(\Lambda)
are clearly different.

Despite the fact that a microframing is not always a framing, we can still discuss two simple cases where these notions agree. For connected Legendrians, there is always an appropriate choice of a framing in the sense of Definition
B.31
that can be understood as simply choosing base point and specifying a trivialization of the microstalks at the base point.

Lemma B.33
.

Let
\Lambda\subset T^{*,\infty}\Sigma
be a Legendrian knot equipped with Maslov potential. Let
\mathcal{M}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
be the moduli space of microlocal rank
r
sheaves with acyclic stalk in a specific stratum
V
of
\Sigma\backslash\pi(\Lambda)
. Let
p\in\Lambda
be a base point such that
\pi(p)
is adjacent to
V
, and
U
be a small neighbourhood of
\pi(p)
such that
\pi(\Lambda)\cap U
is smoothly embedded. Then there exists a framing data
(U,\mathcal{F}_{U})
such that
\mathcal{M}^{\textit{fr}}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}\cong\mathcal{M}^{\mu,\textit{fr}}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
.

Proof.

Consider
\mathcal{F}=\Bbbk^{r}_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}[d(p)]
when the codirection of
p
is pointing toward the stratum
V
and
\mathcal{F}=\Bbbk^{r}_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}}[d(p)-1]
otherwise, where
d(p)\in\mathbb{Z}
is the Maslov potential. Then for any
\mathcal{F}\in Sh^{b}_{\Lambda}(\Sigma)
of microlocal rank
r
, there is always an isomorphism
\mathcal{F}_{U}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{F}
and the choice of the isomorphism is equivalent to the the
t:m_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{F})_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Bbbk^{r}
.
∎

Remark B.34
.

Reduction of base points and the relation between our choice of base points and the choice in
[
65
]
*Section 3.2 for Legendrian
(n,k)
-torus links
\Lambda_{n,k}\subset T^{*,\infty}\mathbb{D}^{2}
with maximal Thurston-Bennequin numbers (where
\Lambda_{n,k}
are realized as Legendrian satellites of the outward unit conormal bundle of a disk following Example
2.9
) is as follows. In
[
65
]
*Section 3.2 the base points are chosen so that their front projection adjacent to the unbounded stratum and the strata corresponding to white vertices in the
(n+k)
-gon in Figure
9
). The stalk in the unbounded stratum is assigned to be acyclic. Hence in that case the framing data is also equivalent to the microframing data.

The above case is not sufficient for applications. Now we focus on Legendrian braid closures and discuss the case of multiple base points.

Proposition B.35
.

Let
\Lambda\subset T^{*,\infty}\Sigma
be a Legendrian positive braid closure (either cylindrical closure or rainbow closure) equipped with Maslov potential,
D\subset\Sigma
an open disk such that
\Lambda\cap T^{*}D
consist of
n
parallel strands. Let
T=\{p_{1},...,p_{n}\}\subset\Lambda
be a collection of base points in
D
, one on each strand,
U_{j}
a sufficiently small neighbourhood of
\pi(p_{j})\in\Sigma
and
U=\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}U_{j}
. Then there exists a framing data such that
\mathcal{M}^{\textit{fr}}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}\cong\mathcal{M}^{\mu,\textit{fr}}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
.

Figure 66.
The Legendrian positive braid closure
\Lambda\subset T^{*,\infty}\Sigma
, the open disk
D\subset\Sigma
specified in the proposition, the base points
T=\{p_{1},\dots,p_{n}\}\subset\Lambda
which determine the microframing of sheaves, and the small open neighbourhoods
U=\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}U_{j}
around projections of base points which determine the framing of sheaves.

Proof.

Given a Legendrian positive
n
-braid closure
\Lambda
and suppose
\mathcal{F}\in Sh^{b}_{\Lambda}(\Sigma)
a microlocal rank 1 sheaf. Fix the base point
p_{i}
lying on the
i
-th strand of
\Lambda
and a small contractible neighbourhood
U_{i}
of
\pi(p_{i})
. Consider the rectangular regions
D_{i}\,(0\leq i\leq n)
lying in between the
(i-1)
and
i
-th strand, as shown in Figure
56
. Since the stalks of
\mathcal{F}
at marked points
\{x_{1},...,x_{m}\}\subset\Sigma
are acyclic and the Maslov potential is fixed, following Example
5.3
we know that

m_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{F})_{p_{i}}\simeq\mathrm{Cone}(\Gamma(D_{i-1},\mathcal{F})\rightarrow\Gamma(D_{i},\mathcal{F}))\simeq\Bbbk^{r},

and hence by induction
\Gamma(D_{i},\mathcal{F})\simeq\Bbbk^{i}
. Since
D_{i}
are contractible, we have
\mathcal{F}_{D_{i}}\simeq\Bbbk^{i}_{D_{i}}
.

Define
\mathcal{F}_{i}\in Sh^{b}_{\Lambda\cap T^{*,\infty}U_{i}}(U_{i})
to be the (unique) microlocal rank 1 sheaf such that

\Gamma(U_{i}\cap D_{i-1},\mathcal{F})=\Bbbk^{(i-1)r},\,\,\Gamma(U_{i}\cap D_{i},\mathcal{F})=\Bbbk^{ir},

and the map
\Gamma(U_{i}\cap D_{i-1},\mathcal{F})\rightarrow\Gamma(U_{i}\cap D_{i},\mathcal{F})
is the inclusion of the first
(i-1)r
coordinates. We claim that there is a bijection between the trivialization data of the microstalk

t_{i}:\,m_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{F})_{p_{i}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Bbbk^{r}

and the pairs
(\mathcal{F}_{i},\varphi_{i})
consisting of
\mathcal{F}_{i}\,(1\leq i\leq n)
and specified quasi-isomorphisms

\varphi_{i}:\,\mathcal{F}|_{U_{i}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{F}_{i}|_{U_{i}}.

First, suppose that the data
t_{i}\,(1\leq i\leq n)
are given. Then we prove by induction that there is a unique quasi-isomorphism

T_{i}^{-}:\,\Gamma(U_{i}\cap D_{i-1},\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Bbbk^{(i-1)r},\,\,\,T_{i}^{+}:\,\Gamma(U_{i}\cap D_{i},\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Bbbk^{ir}.

Then since
U_{i}\cap D_{i-1},U_{i}\cap D_{i}
are contractible, there are canonical quasi-isomorphisms

\mathcal{F}|_{U_{i}\cap D_{i-1}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{F}_{i-1}|_{U_{i}\cap D_{i-1}},\,\,\,\mathcal{F}|_{U_{i}\cap D_{i}}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{i}|_{U_{i}\cap D_{i}},

which uniquely determine quasi-isomorphisms
\varphi_{i-1}:\,\mathcal{F}|_{U_{i}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{F}_{i}|_{U_{i}}.
Therefore, given the trivializations of microstalks
t_{i}\,(1\leq i\leq n)
, to get unique choices of
\varphi_{i}\,(1\leq i\leq n)
, it suffices to prove by induction that the choices of
T_{i}^{\pm}\,(1\leq i\leq n)
are uniquely determined by
t_{i}\,(1\leq i\leq n)
. When
i=0
this is the data

T_{1}^{-}:\,\Gamma(U_{1}\cap D_{0},\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\sim}0,

which is obviously unique. Suppose
T_{i}^{-}
is uniquely determined. Then it follows from the five lemma that there exists a unique quasi-isomorphism
T_{i}^{+}
such that the diagram commutes

\textstyle{\Gamma(U_{i}\cap D_{i-1},\mathcal{F})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{i}^{-}}
\textstyle{\Gamma(U_{i}\cap D_{i},\mathcal{F})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{i}^{+}}
\textstyle{m_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{F})_{p_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 15.0pt+1}
\scriptstyle{t_{i}}
\textstyle{\Bbbk^{(i-1)r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bbbk^{ir}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bbbk^{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 15.0pt+1}
.

Suppose now that
T_{i}^{+}:\Gamma(U_{i}\cap D_{i},\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Bbbk^{ir}
is uniquely determined, then it follows from the fact that
D_{i},U_{i}\cap D_{i},U_{i+1}\cap D_{i}
are contractible that there are canonical quasi-isomorphisms

T_{i+1}^{-}:\Gamma(U_{i+1}\cap D_{i},\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gamma(D_{i},\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gamma(U_{i}\cap D_{i},\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Bbbk^{ir}.

Therefore, the data
t_{i}\,(1\leq i\leq n)
uniquely determines
T_{i}^{\pm}\,(0\leq i\leq n)
and hence the framing data
\varphi_{i}\,(1\leq i\leq n)
.

Next, assume that the data
\varphi_{i}\,(1\leq i\leq n)
are given. Then from the previous discussion, we know that the data
T_{i}^{\pm}\,(0\leq i\leq n)
are thus uniquely given. Hence it follows from the five lemma that there exists a unique trivialization

t_{i}:\,m_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{F})_{p_{i}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Bbbk^{r}

that makes the diagram commutes. This implies that the bijection we have claimed.
∎

Remark B.36
.

Consider Legendrian
(n,k)
-torus links with maximal Thurston-Bennequin invariant, as in Subsection
2.1.1
). The framing data in
[
65
]
*Section 3.2 is equivalent to the microframing data at
(n+k)
base points adjacent to the
(n+k)
vertices coming from the plabic graph on the
(n+k)
-gon by Lemma
B.33
, while here we only choose
n
base points. It is not difficult to check that when we move the
(n+k)
base points counterclockwise to the region
D
as in Proposition
B.35
there will be at least one base point on each strand in
\Lambda_{n,k}\cap T^{*,\infty}D
. Therefore there is a forgetful map from the framed moduli of sheaves in
[
65
]
*Section 3.2 to our framed moduli space, whose fiber is an algebraic torus
(\Bbbk^{\times})^{k}
.

B.4.2.
Microlocal merodromies of sheaves

Our goal in this section is to geometrically introduce the notion of microlocal merodromies along a relative 1-cycle and thus define local coordinate functions on the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{r}^{\mu,\textit{fr}}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
; such construction was implicitly mentioned in
[
65
]
*Section 2.4 & 5.3, up to signs, and appeared in detail in
[
9
]
.

Let
\Lambda\subset T^{*,\infty}\Sigma
be a Legendrian link with Maslov potential. For a microframed sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{M}_{r}^{\mu,\textit{fr}}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
, we have specified trivializations of microstalks at base points on
\Lambda_{i}
. Given a Lagrangian filling
L
of
\Lambda
, a relative 1-cycle in
H_{1}(L,\Lambda;\mathbb{Z})
could end at any point in
\Lambda
. Therefore we need to choose paths in
\Lambda
that cap off the relative 1-cycles.

Definition B.37
.

Let
\Sigma
be a surface with marked points, and
\Lambda=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{m}\Lambda_{i}\subset T^{*,\infty}\Sigma
be a Legendrian link with a chosen relative spin structure
s
and a
\pi_{0}
-surjective set of base points
T=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{m}T_{i}
, i.e. each component
\Lambda_{i}
is equipped with a non-empty set of base points
T_{i}
.

For a base point
p\in\Lambda_{i}
, the capping path
c_{p}
is the smooth path on
\Lambda_{i}
that goes following the orientation of
\Lambda_{i}
, starting from
p
and ending at some
q
without intersecting any other base points.

Remark B.38
.

Note that by Lemma
B.8
, when choosing a spin structure, we fix the a section
s
of the 2-skeleton from
\Lambda
to
\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}}
. Therefore, the capping paths are equipped with liftings
s\circ c_{p}
in the relative oriented frame bundle
\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}}
.

Using capping paths, it follows that, for a sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{M}_{r}^{\mu,\textit{fr}}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
, the microlocalization
m_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{F})
is uniquely trivialized in each component of
\Lambda\backslash P
. Conversely, given a trivialization of
m_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{F})
in each of these connected component, which is an open interval from
p
to
q
), we obtain a trivialization of the microstalk on the rightmost endpoint
q
. Consequently, we conclude the following.

Proposition B.39
.

Let
\Lambda\subset T^{*,\infty}\Sigma
be a Legendrian link with a spin structure
s
and a
\pi_{0}
-surjective set of base points
T
. Then points in
\mathcal{M}_{r}^{\mu,\textit{fr}}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
consist of the data
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{M}_{r}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
together with a trivialization of
m_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{F})
on each connected component of
\Lambda\backslash T
.

Note that this is the definition of decorated moduli space of sheaves in
[
9
]
*Definition 2.37.

Now consider any decorated sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{M}_{r}^{\mu,\textit{fr}}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
. Suppose
\mathcal{F}
is the sheaf quantization of a twisted local system on the Lagrangian filling
L
(with Legendrian lift
\widetilde{L}
). By Theorem
B.20
,
\mathcal{F}
is the proper push forward of a sheaf quantization
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathcal{M}_{r}(\Sigma\times\mathbb{R},\widetilde{L})_{0}
. We want to introduce the notion of microlocal merodromy associated to the sheaf quantization of a (not necessarily embedded) exact Lagrangian filling.

Definition B.40
.

Let
\Lambda\subset T^{*,\infty}\Sigma
be a Legendrian link with the null-cobordant spin structure and a
\pi_{0}
-surjective set of base points
T\subset\Lambda
and capping paths, and
\widetilde{L}\subset T^{*,\infty}(\Sigma\times\mathbb{R})
a Legendrian surface with boundary
\Lambda
with a spin structure
s
. Consider a decorated microlocal rank
r
sheaf
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathcal{M}_{r}^{\mu,\textit{fr}}(\Sigma\times\mathbb{R},\widetilde{L})_{0}
.

Let
\gamma\in H_{1}(L\backslash T,\Lambda\backslash T;\mathbb{Z})
be a relative cycle connecting
p
to
q
and
c_{p},c_{q}
be capping paths of
p,q
. By definition, the microlocal merodromy of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
along
\gamma
is the composition of parallel transport maps of the microstalks

t_{q}\circ\phi_{s\circ{c}_{q}}\circ\phi_{s\circ{\gamma}}\circ\phi_{s\circ{c}_{p}}^{-1}\circ t_{p}^{-1}:\,\Bbbk^{r}\xrightarrow{\sim}m_{L}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}})_{p_{ij}}\xrightarrow{\sim}m_{L}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}})_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}m_{L}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}})_{q}\xrightarrow{\sim}m_{L}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}})_{q_{ij^{\prime}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Bbbk^{r}.

The values of microlocal merodromies depend on the choice of the spin structure
s
, and the lifting of the paths in the relative oriented frame bundle. The reason for the spin structure on
\Lambda
to be null cobordant is because it should coincide with the restriction of the spin structure on the surface
\widetilde{L}
.

Remark B.41
.

Consider the case
r=1
. When
\widetilde{L}
is the Legendrian lift of an embedded Lagrangian
L
, then sheaf quantization of the Lagrangian filling
L
determines a torus chart in the framed moduli of sheaves by microlocal merodromies

H^{1}(L\backslash T,\Lambda\backslash T;\Bbbk^{\times})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mu,\textit{fr}}(\Sigma\times\mathbb{R},\widetilde{L})_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mu,\textit{fr}}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}.

The microlocal merodromy map is a regular function on a Zariski open set of
\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mu,\textit{fr}}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
. In Section
6.1
we explain that they can actually be extended to regular functions on the whole space
\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mu,\textit{fr}}(\Sigma,\Lambda)_{0}
.

Remark B.42
.

If
\Lambda
is a Legendrian link with
n
base points, at least one in each connected component, and
L
a connected Lagrangian filling, then there is an exact sequence

0\rightarrow H_{1}(L,\Lambda;\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow H_{1}(L\backslash T,\Lambda\backslash T;\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow\widetilde{H}_{0}(T;\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow 0.

The exact sequence splits and hence we obtain the isomorphism

H_{1}(L\backslash T,\Lambda\backslash T;\Bbbk^{\times})\cong H_{1}(L,\Lambda;\Bbbk^{\times})\times(\Bbbk^{\times})^{n-1}.

Definition
B.40
can be a priori difficult to work with in practice. However, since the choice of spin structures can be reduced to the choice of sign curves, microlocal merodromies can actually be computed in examples, as shown in Section
5.3
.
